) and (
6.4
) from Lemma
6.2
yield

p_{\kappa_{i},L}\prod_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j\leq r\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}}\,\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}\lesssim_{k,L}\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2\kappa_{i}+1}\prod_{j\neq i}\mathfrak{e}^{-2\kappa_{j}}\mathfrak{d}\lesssim_{k,L}\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}\mathfrak{d}.

Here we use
\kappa_{i}\geq 1
to apply (
6.2
).
In the contribution to
h_{\geq 1}
from
B
, we sometimes have
\kappa_{0}=0
.
If
\kappa_{0}\geq 1
, similar manipulations yield

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}p_{\kappa_{0},L})\prod_{j}\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]},\,(\partial_{x}\psi_{\kappa_{0},[L]})p_{k_{i},L}\prod_{j\neq i}\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,L}\mathfrak{d}^{-3}\mathfrak{e}^{2}\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,L}\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}\mathfrak{d}.

Here we used (
3.5
) to conclude that
\mathfrak{d}^{-3}\mathfrak{e}^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{d}
.
If
\kappa_{0}=0
, we use (
5.5
) to similarly write

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}p_{0,L})\prod_{j}\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,L}\nu^{-o_{L}(1)}\mathfrak{d}^{L-1}\prod_{j}\mathfrak{e}^{-2\kappa_{j}}\lesssim_{k,L}\nu^{-o_{L}(1)}\mathfrak{d}^{L-1}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,L}\nu^{-o_{L}(1)}\mathfrak{d}^{L}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}\mathfrak{d}.

Now
\nu^{-o_{L}(1)}\mathfrak{d}^{L}\lesssim_{L}\nu^{L\beta/3-o_{L}(1)}\lesssim_{L}\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}
, so this term is bounded like the rest.

We have now treated all terms in
h_{\geq 1}
with exactly one factor of
p
, and those with multiple factors satisfy identical bounds.
As usual,
\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}
adds a factor of
\mathfrak{e}^{-m}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n}
, so the lemma follows.
∎

6.2.
Partial sum equation

We next show that
\psi_{k,[L]}
approximately satisfies (
3.3
).
Recall that
h_{0}
denotes the terms in the right side of (
6.3
) with no factors of
p
.

Lemma 6.4
.

For all
k,L\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist
h^{\textnormal{res}}
and
F^{\textnormal{res}}
such that

(6.5)

\partial_{t}\psi_{k,[L]}+\partial_{x}[A({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})\psi_{k,[L]}]=\partial_{x}h_{0}+\partial_{x}h^{\textnormal{res}}+F^{\textnormal{res}}

and for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}h_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\textnormal{res}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,L,m,n}\nu^{(L-1/2)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n},

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}F_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\textnormal{res}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,L,m,n}\nu^{(L-1/2)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k-m}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n},

and

(6.6)

\left|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}(\partial_{x}h_{1}^{\textnormal{res}}+F_{1}^{\textnormal{res}})\right|\lesssim_{k,L,m,n}\mathfrak{d}^{-2}\nu^{(L-1/2)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n}.

Proof.

We first analyze the nonshocking components.
Using Proposition
4.3
, we can write

\partial_{t}\omega_{k,\ell}=-\partial_{x}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq r\leq\ell\\
\kappa_{0}+\ldots+\kappa_{r}=k\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\psi_{\kappa,\alpha}+\partial_{x}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq r\leq\ell-1\\
\kappa_{0}+\ldots+\kappa_{r}=k-1\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\psi_{\kappa,\alpha}-\nu^{-1}\delta_{k,1}F_{\ell}^{\omega}.

Recall the notation
\left|\alpha\right|\coloneqq\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}
and
\#\alpha\coloneqq r
.
Then summing over
0\leq\ell\leq L
, we can compactly write

\partial_{t}\omega_{k,[L]}=-\partial_{x}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\kappa\right|=k\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha\leq L\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\psi_{\kappa,\alpha}+\partial_{x}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\kappa\right|=k-1\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha\leq L-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\psi_{\kappa,\alpha}-\nu^{-1}\delta_{k,1}F_{[L]}^{\omega}.

To compare this with
h_{0}
in (
6.3
), we use the smallness of
{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}
in
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}
to expand
\mathrm{D}^{r}A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})
(after projecting onto the nonshocking subspace
\mathcal{V}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}
).
Using Proposition
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r}A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})}{(r+1)!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{L-r}\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r+s}A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}(0)}{(r+1)!\,s!}({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})^{s}+\mathcal{O}_{L}\big(|{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}|^{L-r+1}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{L-r}\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r+s}A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}(0)}{(r+1)!\,s!}\psi_{0,[L]}^{s}+\mathcal{O}_{L}\big(|\psi_{0,[L]}|^{L-r+1}\big).

Now
\frac{1}{(r+1)!\,s!}=\frac{1}{(r+s+1)!}\binom{r+s+1}{s}
, and the binomial coefficient counts the number of ways of inserting
s
factors of
\psi_{0,[L]}
into a product
\prod_{j=0}^{r+s}\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}
.
Replacing
r+s
by
r
, we can therefore write

\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}r\geq 0,\,\kappa_{j}\geq 1\\
\kappa_{0}+\ldots+\kappa_{r}=k\end{subarray}}}\,\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r}A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})}{(r+1)!}\prod_{j=0}^{r}\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}=\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\#\kappa\leq L\\
\left|\kappa\right|=k\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\psi_{\kappa,[L]}+\mathcal{O}_{L}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\#\kappa=L+1\\
\left|\kappa\right|=k\end{subarray}}\prod_{j}|\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}|\Big).

Expanding each
\psi_{\kappa,[L]}=\sum_{\ell\leq L}\psi_{\kappa,\ell}
, we find

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}r\geq 0,\,\kappa_{j}\geq 1\\
\kappa_{0}+\ldots+\kappa_{r}=k\end{subarray}}}\,\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r}A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})}{(r+1)!}

\displaystyle\prod_{j=0}^{r}\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}-\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\kappa\right|=k\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha\leq L\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\psi_{\kappa,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,L}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\#\alpha\leq L,\,\alpha_{j}\leq L\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha>L\\
\left|\kappa\right|=k\end{subarray}}\prod_{j}|\psi_{\kappa_{j},\alpha_{j}}|+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\#\kappa=L+1\\
\left|\kappa\right|=k\end{subarray}}\prod_{j}|\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}|.

Using Lemma
5.11
, we find

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\#\kappa=\#\alpha\leq L\\
\left|\kappa\right|=k,\,\alpha_{j}\leq L\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha>L\end{subarray}}\prod_{j}|\psi_{\kappa_{j},\alpha_{j}}|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,k,L}\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}.

(Though the lemma is stated for
\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}
, the proof controls the product above.)
Next, (
3.5
) and (
6.4
) yield

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\#\kappa=L+1\\
\left|\kappa\right|=k\end{subarray}}\prod_{j}|\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}|\lesssim_{k,L}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\#\kappa=L+1\\
\left|\kappa\right|=k\end{subarray}}\prod_{j}\mathfrak{d}\mathfrak{e}^{-2\kappa_{j}}\lesssim_{k,L}\mathfrak{d}^{L+2}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k}\lesssim_{k,L}\nu^{\beta L/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}.

Combining these bounds, we have thus shown:

\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}r\geq 0,\,\kappa_{j}\geq 1\\
\kappa_{0}+\ldots+\kappa_{r}=k\end{subarray}}}\,\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r}A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})}{(r+1)!}\prod_{j=0}^{r}\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}=\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\kappa\right|=k\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha\leq L\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\psi_{\kappa,\alpha}+\mathcal{O}_{k,L}\big(\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}\big).

In a similar fashion, we can check that

\hskip 14.0pt\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}r\geq 0,\,\kappa_{\geq 1}\hskip 0.5pt\geq 1\\
\kappa_{0}+\ldots+\kappa_{r}=k-1\end{subarray}}}\,\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r}B({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})}{r!}(\partial_{x}\psi_{\kappa_{0},[L]})\prod_{j=1}^{r}\psi_{\kappa_{j},[L]}=\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\kappa\right|=k-1\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha\leq L-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\psi_{\kappa,\alpha}+\mathcal{O}_{k,L}\big(\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}\big).

The details are sufficiently similar that we do not repeat them.
We let
h_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\text{res}}
denote the sum of the error terms in the above two displays.

Finally, when
k=1
, (
3.5
), Lemma
5.3
, and
\beta>(2+\varepsilon)^{-1}
, imply that

\displaystyle F_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\text{res}}\coloneqq-\nu^{-1}\delta_{k,1}F_{[L]}^{\omega}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{L}\nu^{-1-o_{L}(1)}\mathfrak{d}^{L}\lesssim_{L}\nu^{(2+\varepsilon)\beta-1-o_{L}(1)}\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{L}\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon)\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2}.

Now (
6.5
) follows from Proposition
4.3
and (
6.3
).
Derivative bounds follow as usual.

We take a similar approach with the shocking component.
Using Lemma
5.11
, we pick up an additional factor of
\mathfrak{d}
in
h_{1}^{\text{res}}
relative to the nonshocking estimates.
Applying
\partial_{x}
, we obtain (
6.6
).
∎

6.3.
Integrating residue equation

Now our matching estimate is a simple consequence of the hyperbolic estimate Proposition
5.5
we stated earlier.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

Fix
k,L\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}
be the mean of
\varepsilon
and
\beta^{-1}-2
, so
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)
and
\beta>(2+\varepsilon^{\prime})^{-1}
.
Proceeding by induction, we assume that (
6.2
) holds for all
k^{\prime}<k
with
\varepsilon^{\prime}
in place of
\varepsilon
.
Note that
p_{k,L}=0
on
\Gamma_{\beta}
.
Combining Lemmas
6.3
and
6.4
, we see that
\nu^{-(L-3\varepsilon^{\prime})\beta/3}p_{k,L}
satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition
5.5
with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{I}_{\beta}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1/2}
and
\Sigma=\Gamma_{\beta}
.
By said proposition,

|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}p_{k,L}|\lesssim_{k,L,m,n}\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|\nu^{(L-3\varepsilon^{\prime})\beta/3}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n}.

In
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1/2}
,
\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|\asymp\left|\log\nu\right|
.
Using
\left|\log\nu\right|\nu^{-\varepsilon^{\prime}/\beta}\lesssim\nu^{-\varepsilon/\beta}
, we obtain (
6.2
).
∎

It remains to prove the hyperbolic estimate.

Proof of Proposition
5.5
.

For the reader’s convenience, we recall the setting.
Let
p
solve
\partial_{t}p+\partial_{x}[A({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})p]=\partial_{x}h+F
in a causally closed domain
\mathcal{D}
with Cauchy hypersurface
\Sigma
, and suppose

(6.7)

|\nabla^{n}(p|_{\Sigma}|)\lesssim_{n}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n}.

Setting
a_{k}=\textup{{1}}(k\geq 2)
, assume

(6.8)

|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}h^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}|\lesssim_{m,n}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n},\quad|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}F^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}|\lesssim_{m,n}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k-m}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n},

and

(6.9)

|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}(\partial_{x}h^{1}+F^{1})|\lesssim_{m,n}\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|^{a_{k}}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n-2}.

We wish to show that

(6.10)

|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}p|\lesssim_{m,n}\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|\mathfrak{e}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{d}^{-3n}.

We first suppose
m=n=0
and employ the following bootstrap assumption for a time
t_{\mathrm{b}}
:

(6.11)

|p|\lesssim_{m,n}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+9/10}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{D}\cap\{t<t_{\mathrm{b}}\}.

We show that the stronger estimate (
6.10
) holds until
t_{\mathrm{b}}
.
By continuity, we can push
t_{\mathrm{b}}
to
0
and complete the proof.

To avoid derivative loss, we work with the left eigenvectors
l
and eigenvalues
\lambda
associated to the variable matrix
A({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})
rather than the frozen matrix
A(0)
.
For the sake of brevity, in the following we implicitly compose
A
,
l
,
\lambda
, and their derivatives with
{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}
.
So, for example,
A
represents
A({\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}})
.
We note that Proposition
5.1
implies that

(6.12)

\partial_{t}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}\lesssim\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}\lesssim\mathfrak{d}^{-2}.

First fix a nonshocking index
2\leq I^{\prime}\leq N
.
Let
p^{I^{\prime}}\coloneqq l^{I^{\prime}}p
, and similarly
h^{I^{\prime}}
and
F^{I^{\prime}}
.
Commuting the
p
-equation with
l^{I^{\prime}}
, we find

(6.13)

(\partial_{t}+\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}\partial_{x})p^{I^{\prime}}=[\mathrm{D}\ell_{I^{\prime}}\partial_{t}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}+\mathrm{D}\ell_{I^{\prime}}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}A-\mathrm{D}\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}]p+l^{I^{\prime}}\partial_{x}h+F^{I^{\prime}}.

Let
M
denote the matrix coefficient of
p
in brackets.
To renormalize, we write

l^{I^{\prime}}\partial_{x}h=\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}^{-1}l^{I^{\prime}}\partial_{x}h=\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}\partial_{x}(\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}^{-1}h^{I^{\prime}})-\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}\mathrm{D}(\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}^{-1}l^{I^{\prime}})\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}h.

Then we can write (
6.13
) as

(\partial_{t}+\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}\partial_{x})(p-\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}h^{I^{\prime}})=Mp-\partial_{t}(\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}^{-1}h^{I^{\prime}})-\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}\mathrm{D}(\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}^{-1}l^{I^{\prime}})\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}h+F^{I^{\prime}}.

Because
I^{\prime}
is a nonshocking index, strict hyperbolicity implies that
\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}^{-1}
is uniformly bounded in a neighborhood of the origin.
Combining (
6.8
) and (
6.12
) with the bootstrap (
6.11
), we see that

(\partial_{t}+\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}\partial_{x})(p-\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}h^{I^{\prime}})\lesssim\mathfrak{d}^{-2}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+9/10}+\mathfrak{e}^{-2k}

and
\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}h^{I^{\prime}}\lesssim\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}
.
Combining (
5.17
) and (
5.18
) of Lemma
5.8
and (
6.7
), we obtain

p^{I^{\prime}}\lesssim\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1/3+9/10}+\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}\lesssim\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}.

We have thus improved on the bootstrap in the nonshocking component.

Similar reasoning in the shocking component yields

(\partial_{t}+\lambda_{(1)}\partial_{x})p^{1}=[\mathrm{D}l^{1}\partial_{t}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}+\mathrm{D}l^{1}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}A-\mathrm{D}\lambda_{(1)}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}]p+l^{1}\partial_{x}h+F^{1}.

Recall the right eigenvectors
r_{I}
of
A
.
Using our bounds on the nonshocking components and
\lambda_{(1)}\asymp\sigma_{0,0}\lesssim\mathfrak{d}
, we have

\mathrm{D}l^{1}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}Ap=\mathrm{D}l^{1}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}(\lambda_{(1)}p^{1}r_{1}+\lambda_{(I^{\prime})}p^{I^{\prime}}r_{I^{\prime}})=\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{d}^{-1})p^{1}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{d}^{-2}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}).

Next, we use Proposition
5.1
to write

-\mathrm{D}\lambda_{(1)}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}p=-\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{x}\sigma_{0,0}p^{1}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{d}^{-1}p^{1})+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{d}^{-2}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}).

Finally, (
6.8
), (
6.9
), and (
6.12
) yield

l^{1}\partial_{x}h=\partial_{x}h^{1}-\mathrm{D}l^{1}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}h\lesssim\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|^{a_{k}}\mathfrak{d}^{-2}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}.

Collecting these observations and using (
6.9
), we have

(\partial_{t}+\lambda_{(1)}\partial_{x})p^{1}=-\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{x}\sigma_{0,0}p^{1}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{d}^{-1}p^{1})+\mathcal{O}(\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|^{a_{k}}\mathfrak{d}^{-2}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}).

Now Proposition
5.1
implies that
\lambda_{(1)}=\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\sigma_{0,0}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{d}^{2})
.
Following the logic leading to (
5.10
), we multiply by the integrating factor
\mathfrak{d}^{2}
to obtain

(\partial_{t}+\lambda_{(1)}\partial_{x})(\mathfrak{d}^{2}p^{1})=\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{d}p^{1})+\mathcal{O}(\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|^{a_{k}}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}).

The bootstrap (
6.11
) implies that the second term on the right dominates the first, so in fact

(\partial_{t}+\lambda_{(1)}\partial_{x})(\mathfrak{d}^{2}p^{1})\lesssim\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|^{a_{k}}\mathfrak{e}^{-2k+1}.

Using (
5.19
) or (
5.20
) of Lemma
5.8
(if
k\geq 2
or
k=1
) and (
6.7
), we conclude that

\mathfrak{d}^{2}p^{1}\lesssim\left|\log\mathfrak{e}\right|\mathfrak{e}^{-2k}.

The desired bound follows from (
3.5
).
Having improved on the bootstrap, we see that in fact (
6.10
) holds throughout
\mathcal{D}
.

Derivative estimates follow from analogous calculations.
In the nonshocking case, we use a variant of
\mathbf{R}_{n}
from Lemma
5.7
to renormalize
\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}p
.
Bounds on the derivatives along the Cauchy hypersurface
\Sigma
follow from the reasoning leading to (
5.35
).
We omit these similar calculations.
∎

7.
Inner estimates and inner-grid matching

We now show that summing the grid
(\psi_{k,\ell})
in
k
asymptotically yields the inner expansion
\psi_{\ell}
.
We work in inner coordinates, and therefore study the inner grid
\Psi_{k,\ell}
defined in (
4.21
).
Recalling the partial sum
\Psi_{[K],\ell}\coloneqq\sum_{k\leq K}\Psi_{k,\ell}
and (
4.15
), we expect
{\Psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}\approx\Psi_{[K],\ell}
in the matching region
\nu^{1/2}\ll\mathfrak{e}\lesssim\nu^{\beta}
when
K\gg 1
.

It will be convenient to extend statements to the “true inner zone” where
\mathfrak{e}\lesssim\nu^{1/2}
.
However, the terms
\Psi_{k,\ell}
are singular at the origin while
{\Psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}
remains regular.
To circumvent this difficulty, we cut off
\Psi_{k,\ell}
in the true inner zone.
Let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}(T,X)\coloneqq\nu^{-1/4}\mathfrak{d}\big(\nu^{1/2}T,\nu^{3/4}X\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}(T,X),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}(T,X)\coloneqq\nu^{-1/2}\mathfrak{e}\big(\nu^{1/2}T,\nu^{3/4}X\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\left|T\right|^{2}+\nu^{1/2}\left|X\right|^{2}\big)^{1/2},

noting that
\mathfrak{E}
depends implicitly on
\nu
because
\mathfrak{e}
is not a homogeneous function under our cubic convention.
The true inner region
\{\mathfrak{e}\lesssim\nu^{1/2}\}
corresponds to
\{\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1\}
.
Let
\theta\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a smooth cutoff function satisfying
\theta|_{[0,1/2]}\equiv 0
and
\theta|_{[1,\infty)}\equiv 1
.
We write
\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}\coloneqq\theta\circ\mathfrak{E}
and
\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}\coloneqq\theta\circ\mathfrak{D}
and define the cutoff grid

{\Psi}_{k,\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}\Psi_{0,\ell}&\text{if }k=0,\\
\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}\Psi_{k,\ell}&\text{if }k\geq 1\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad{\Psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}\coloneqq\sum_{k\leq K}{\Psi}_{k,\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}.

We emphasize that we use a less aggressive cutoff
\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}
for the inviscid terms
\Psi_{0,\ell}
, which are known quite precisely through Proposition
5.1
.

Given
K\in\mathbb{N}
, we construct solutions of the inner equations that agree closely with
{\Psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}
away from the cutoff regions.
There is one wrinkle, however.
Recalling (
4.19
), we will only employ the inner expansion where
\mathfrak{e}\leq\frac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}
.
Our shocking inner solution
{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}
will solve (
4.14
) exactly where
\mathfrak{e}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}
, but with additional forcing
G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}
where
\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}<\mathfrak{e}<\mathsf{R}\nu^{\beta}
.
So our solutions will satisfy

(7.1)

\displaystyle[\nu^{1/4}\partial_{T}+A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}(0)

\displaystyle]\partial_{X}{\Omega}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}=-\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq r\leq\ell\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}+\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq r\leq\ell-1\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}},

\displaystyle\partial_{T}{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{x}({\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}})

(7.2)

\displaystyle-B_{1}^{1}(0

\displaystyle)\partial_{X}^{2}{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}=-\partial_{X}\sum^{*}_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq r\leq\ell+1\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r+1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{1}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}+\partial_{X}\sum^{*}_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq r\leq\ell\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{1}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}+G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}.

With this notation, we can state the main result of the section.

Proposition 7.1
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/6]
and
\beta\in(\tfrac{1}{2+\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2})
.
For all
K\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a solution
({\Psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}})_{\ell}
of (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) in
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}
with
G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}\equiv 0
where
\mathfrak{e}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}
such that for all
\ell,m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

(7.3)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{T}^{m}

\displaystyle\partial_{X}^{n}({\Psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}-{\Psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)-2\beta/3-\varepsilon\beta+1/2}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-2m-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n}.

We follow the approach to outer matching in Section
6
.
Let
P_{K,\ell}\coloneqq{\Psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}-{\Psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}
denote the difference of interest with
P=(P^{\Sigma},P^{\Omega})
.
Then we can write (
4.13
) as

(7.4)

\displaystyle({\Omega}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}+P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega})

\displaystyle=-\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq r\leq\ell\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}({\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+P_{K,\alpha})+\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq r\leq\ell-1\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}({\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+P_{K,\alpha})

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\partial_{X}h_{0}+\partial_{X}h_{\geq 1},

where
h_{0}
denotes the collection of terms on the right with no factors of
P
, and
h_{\geq 1}
the collection with at least one factor of
P
.
Similarly, (
4.14
) yields

(7.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{T}(

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\Sigma}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}+P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma})+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{x}[{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}({\Sigma}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}+P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma})]-B_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}^{2}({\Sigma}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}+P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma})

\displaystyle=-\partial_{X}\sum^{*}_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq r\leq\ell+1\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r+1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{1}({\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+P_{K,\alpha})+\partial_{X}\sum^{*}_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq r\leq\ell\\
\alpha_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{r}=\ell-r\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{1}({\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+P_{K,\alpha})+G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon G_{0}+G_{\geq 1}+G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}.

We first inductively show that
h_{\geq 1}
and
G_{\geq 1}
are small.
We then argue that
\partial_{X}h_{0}
and
G_{0}
nearly cancel the partial-sum terms on the left of (
7.4
) and (
7.5
).
Finally, we integrate (
7.4
) and use the comparison principle in (
7.5
) to show that
P
is small.

Above, we assume
\ell\geq 1
.
In the zeroth column (
\ell=0
),
\Psi_{k,0}=(\Sigma_{k,0},0)^{\top}
and we constructed both
(\Sigma_{k,0})
and
{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}
in
[
22
]
.

Proposition 7.2
.

For all
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}|\lesssim_{m,n}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{1-2m-3n}
and

(7.6)

\big|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}({\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}-{\Sigma}_{[K],0}^{\textnormal{cut}})\big|\lesssim_{K,m,n}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-4K-2m-3n-3}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{I}_{\beta}.

This is essentially Corollary 4.3 and Proposition 4.2 in
[
22
]
.
There we did not explicitly treat time derivatives, but the estimate (
7.6
) follows from the evolution equation for
{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}
and induction in
m
.
We note that the region
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}
looks large in the inner coordinates, for there
\mathfrak{E}\lesssim\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)}
.

We also record bounds in the outer coordinates, scaling
{\psi}_{0,\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}
in the natural way.

Corollary 7.3
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/6]
and
\beta\in(\tfrac{1}{2+\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2})
.
For all
K,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

(7.7)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}({\psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}-{\psi}_{0,\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{1+(\ell-1)\beta/3-\varepsilon\beta}(\mathfrak{e}\vee\nu^{1/2})^{-m-1}(\mathfrak{d}\vee\nu^{1/4})^{-3n},

(7.8)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}{\psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K,\ell,m,n}\left|\log\nu\right|^{\ell-1}\nu^{(\ell+1)\beta/3}(\mathfrak{e}\vee\nu^{1/2})^{-m}(\mathfrak{d}\vee\nu^{1/4})^{-3n}

in
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}
.
Moreover, in
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}\setminus\big\{\mathfrak{e}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}\big\}
,

(7.9)

\big|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}({\psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}-\psi_{[K],\ell})\big|\lesssim_{K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{(1-2\beta)K+(\ell+1)\beta/3-m/2-3n/4}.

7.1.
Difference estimates

In this section we control the size of
h_{\geq 1}
in (
7.4
) and
G_{\geq 1}
in (
7.5
).
We use of the following bounds on the partial sums
{\Psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}
.

Lemma 7.4
.

For all
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell,m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, in
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}
we have

(7.10)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}{\Psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K,\ell,m,n}\left|\log\nu\right|^{\ell-1}\nu^{-\ell(1/4-\beta/3)}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2m}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n+1},

(7.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K,\ell,m,n}\left|\log\nu\right|^{\ell-1}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2m}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n}.

Proof.

Thanks to the cutoff, it suffices to treat
\nu^{1/2}\lesssim\mathfrak{e}\lesssim\nu^{\beta}
.
There, Propositions
5.1
and
5.10
yield
\psi_{0,\ell}\lesssim\left|\log\nu\right|^{\ell-1}\nu^{\ell\beta/3}\mathfrak{d}
and
\nu^{k}\psi_{k,\ell}\lesssim\nu^{1/2+(\ell-3/2)\beta/3}\lesssim\nu^{\ell\beta/3+1/4}
, using
\beta<1/2
in the last bound.
Switching to inner coordinates, we multiply by
\nu^{-(\ell+1)/4}
and use
\mathfrak{d}=\nu^{1/4}\mathfrak{D}
to obtain (
7.10
) for
m=n=0
.
Derivative estimates follow in the usual manner.
Because
\mathfrak{D}\lesssim\nu^{-(1/4-\beta/3)}
in
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}
, (
7.11
) follows from (
7.10
).
∎

We now establish an inductive bound on the terms in (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) containing at least one factor of
P
.
Throughout this section, we use the shorthand

(7.12)

\eta\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2\beta}{3}-\varepsilon\beta\in\Big(\frac{1}{12},\frac{1}{6}\Big).

The given bounds follow from the conditions
\beta>\tfrac{1}{2+\varepsilon}
and
\beta<1/2
,
\varepsilon\leq 1/6
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/6],

\beta\in(\tfrac{1}{2+\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2})
, and
K,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
If Proposition
7.1
holds for all
\ell^{\prime}<\ell
, then for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

(7.13)

\big|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}h_{\geq 1}\big|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-2m-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n}.

If moreover Proposition
7.1
holds for
P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}
, then

(7.14)

\big|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}G_{\geq 1}\big|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-2m-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n-2}.

Proof.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/6]
and
\beta\in\big(\tfrac{1}{2+\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2}\big)
.
Choose
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)
such that
\tfrac{1}{2+\varepsilon^{\prime}}
is midway between
\tfrac{1}{2+\varepsilon}
and
\beta
.
Define
\eta^{\prime}>\eta
with
\varepsilon^{\prime}
in place of
\varepsilon
in (
7.12
).
For
1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\ell
, Proposition
7.1
yields

(7.15)

|P_{K,\ell^{\prime}}|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell^{\prime}+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}.

Comparing with (
7.10
) and using
\eta^{\prime}-(1/4-\beta/3)>\eta^{\prime}-1/12>0
, we see that the dominant contributions to
h_{\geq 1}
and
F_{\geq 1}
involve exactly one factor of
P
when
\ell^{\prime}\geq 1
.
Thanks to Proposition
7.2
, the same holds for
\ell^{\prime}=0
.
We therefore only treat terms with exactly one factor of
P
.

First consider the contributions to
h_{\geq 1}
from
\mathcal{A}
in (
7.4
).
Given
1\leq i\leq r
,
Lemma
7.4
yields

P_{K,\alpha_{i}}\prod_{j\neq i}{\Psi}_{[K],\alpha_{j}}^{\textnormal{cut}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\alpha_{i}+1)(1/4-\beta/4)+\eta^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}\prod_{j\neq i}\nu^{-(\alpha_{j}+1)(1/4-\beta/3)-o_{\ell}(1)}

For these terms,
\sum_{j}(\alpha_{j}+1)=\ell+1
, so this simplifies to

P_{K,\alpha_{i}}\prod_{j\neq i}{\Psi}_{[K],\alpha_{j}}^{\textnormal{cut}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/4)+\eta^{\prime}-o_{\ell}(1)}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}.

Now
\eta^{\prime}>\eta
, so the right side of (
7.13
) controls these terms.
For the contributions to
h_{\geq 1}
from
\mathcal{B}
, the derivative
\partial_{X}
is helpful in the inner coordinates (multiplying by
(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3}\lesssim 1
).
Moreover, these terms satisfy
\sum_{j}(\alpha_{j}+1)=\ell
, leaving us with a less singular
\nu
-factor.
So these terms are likewise controlled by the right side of (
7.13
), completing the proof for
m=n=0
.
Derivatives multiply our estimates by factors of
(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-1}
or
(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3}
in the usual fashion.

We now turn to
G_{\geq 1}
in (
7.5
).
First consider contributions from
P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}
, for which we can assume

|P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}.

Hence by Proposition
7.2
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{X}({\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega})\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-2},

\displaystyle\partial_{X}^{2}P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-6}.

Next consider the
\mathcal{A}
contributions to
G_{\geq 1}
in (
7.5
).
First,
\partial_{X}
contributes a factor of
(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3}
.
Second, because
r\geq 1
in that sum, these terms include at least one factor of
{\Psi}_{[K],\ell^{\prime}}^{\textnormal{cut}}
.
We use (
7.10
) for one such factor and (
7.11
) for the rest.
Combining this with (
7.15
) and
\sum_{j}(\alpha_{j}+1)=\ell+2
, we find

P_{K,\alpha_{i}}\prod_{j\neq i}{\Psi}_{[K],\alpha_{j}}^{\textnormal{cut}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-2}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta^{\prime}-o_{\ell}(1)}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-1}.

Compared to our earlier
\Omega
-calculation, the refined estimate (
7.10
) allows us to trade one factor of
\nu^{-(1/4-\beta/3)}
for
\mathfrak{D}\vee 1
and thus overcome the larger value of
\sum_{i}(\alpha_{i}+1)=\ell+2
.
We now use
\eta^{\prime}>\eta
to absorb
\nu^{-o_{\ell}(1)}
.

The
\mathcal{B}
contributions satisfy
\sum_{j}(\alpha_{j}+1)=\ell+1
, so we can bound them using (
7.11
) and (
7.15
), much as in the nonshocking calculation.
We obtain (
7.14
) for
m=n=0
, and derivative estimates routinely follow.
∎

7.2.
Partial sum equation

Next, we show that
{\Omega}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}
and
{\Sigma}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}
approximately solve (
4.13
) and (
4.14
), respectively.
Recall that
h_{0}
and
G_{0}
account for terms with no factors of
P
in (
7.4
) and (
7.5
).

Lemma 7.6
.

For all
K,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist
h^{\textnormal{res}}
,
F^{\textnormal{res}}
, and
G^{\textnormal{res}}
such that

(7.16)

\displaystyle[\nu^{1/4}\partial_{T}+A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}(0)\partial_{X}]{\Omega}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}=\partial_{X}(h_{0}+h^{\textnormal{res}})+F^{\textnormal{res}},

(7.17)

\displaystyle\partial_{T}{\Sigma}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}({\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}{\Sigma}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}})-B_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}^{2}{\Sigma}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}=G_{0}+G^{\textnormal{res}},

and for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}h^{\textnormal{res}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,k,L,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-2m-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n},

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}F^{\textnormal{res}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,k,L,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta+1/4}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\leq 1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n},

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}G^{\textnormal{res}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,k,L,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-2m-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n-2}.

We make use of the following restatement of Lemma
5.11
, which is immediate from the multilinearity of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
.

Lemma 7.7
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/6]
,
\beta\in\big(\tfrac{1}{2+\varepsilon},\tfrac{1}{2}\big)
,
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, and
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

(i)

If
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell
and
\left|\kappa\right|=k
,

|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{T}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\Psi_{\kappa,\alpha}|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,k,\ell,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\mathfrak{E}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{D}^{-3n}.

(ii)

If
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell-1
and
\left|\kappa\right|=k-1
,

|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\Psi_{\kappa,\alpha}|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,k,\ell,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\mathfrak{E}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{D}^{-3n}.

(iii)

If
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell+1
and
\left|\kappa\right|=k
,

|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{1}\Psi_{\kappa,\alpha}|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,k,\ell,m,n}\mathfrak{D}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\mathfrak{E}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{D}^{-3n}.

(iv)

If
\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell
and
\left|\kappa\right|=k-1
,

|\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}\mathcal{B}_{1}\Psi_{\kappa,\alpha}|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,k,\ell,m,n}\mathfrak{D}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\mathfrak{E}^{-2k-m+1}\mathfrak{D}^{-3n}.

Proof of Lemma
7.6
.

The residues in the lemma come from two sources: the cutoff and the fact that the multilinear operators
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
do not commute with partial summation.
We treat cutoff error first.

The inner equations involve the operators
\partial_{T}
,
\partial_{X}\mathcal{A}
, and
\partial_{X}\mathcal{B}
.
We multiply these equations by a cutoff
\theta
and move it through the operators to land on
\Psi_{k,\ell}
, forming
{\Psi}_{k,\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}
.
In the process, we generate commutators of the form
[\theta,\partial_{T}]
,
[\theta,\partial_{X}]\mathcal{A}
, and
\partial_{X}[\theta,\mathcal{A}]
(resp.
\mathcal{B}
).
In the nonshocking component, we allot the first two to
F^{\textnormal{res}}
and the last to
h^{\textnormal{res}}
(as a derivative in
X
).
We group all in
G^{\textnormal{res}}
in the shocking component.

Recall that we use different cutoffs
\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}
and
\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}
for
\Psi_{0,\ell}
and
\Psi_{\geq 1,\ell}
.
These excise the regions
\mathfrak{D}\leq 1/2
and
\mathfrak{E}\leq 1/2
, respectively.
To treat the distinction, let
\Psi_{[1,K],\ell}\coloneqq\Psi_{[K],\ell}-\Psi_{0,\ell}
denote the sum of terms in
\Psi_{[K],\ell}
with
k\geq 1
.
Then
{\Psi}_{0,\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}=\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}\Psi_{0,\ell}
and
{\Psi}_{[1,K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}=\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}\Psi_{[1,K],\ell}
.

First consider the nonshocking component.
Writing (
5.3
) in the inner coordinates and commuting with
\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}
, we have

(7.18)

\nu^{1/4}\partial_{T}{\Omega}_{0,\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}=\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{0,\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\partial_{X}h_{1}+F_{1}+\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}F_{\ell}^{\Omega},

where
F_{\ell}^{\Omega}
denotes the scaling of
F_{\ell}^{\omega}
to inner coordinates,

h_{1}\coloneqq\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell\end{subarray}}}\,[\theta_{\mathfrak{D}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}]\Psi_{0,\alpha},\quad\text{and}\quad F_{1}\coloneqq-\nu^{1/4}[\theta_{\mathfrak{D}},\partial_{T}]\Omega_{0,\ell}+\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell\end{subarray}}}\,[\theta_{\mathfrak{D}},\partial_{X}]\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\Psi_{0,\alpha}.

We can scale Proposition
5.1
to find
|\Psi_{0,\ell}|\lesssim_{\ell}\left|\log\nu\right|^{\ell}
where
\mathfrak{D}\asymp 1
, and likewise for derivatives.
The above commutators are supported where
1/2\leq\mathfrak{D}\leq 1
, so
\left|h_{1}\right|+\left|F_{1}\right|\lesssim_{\ell}\nu^{-o_{\ell}(1)}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{D}\leq 1}
.
Because
\ell\geq 1
,
\eta<(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)
and we can bound this by a multiple of
\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{D}\leq 1}
.

For
k\geq 1
, we write (
4.16
) in inner coordinates and commute with
\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}
to obtain

\displaystyle\nu^{1/4}\partial_{T}{\Omega}_{[1,K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}

\displaystyle=\overbrace{-\nu^{1/4}[\theta_{\mathfrak{E}},\partial_{T}]\Omega_{[1,K],\ell}}^{F_{2}}

(7.19)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell\\
1\leq\left|\kappa\right|\leq K\end{subarray}}}\,\big(\partial_{X}\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{\kappa,\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\overbrace{[\theta_{\mathfrak{E}},\partial_{X}]\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\Psi_{\kappa,\alpha}}^{F_{2}}+\partial_{X}\overbrace{[\theta_{\mathfrak{E}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}]\Psi_{\kappa,\alpha}}^{h_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell-1\\
\left|\kappa\right|\leq K-1\end{subarray}}}\,\big(\partial_{X}\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{\kappa,\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\underbrace{[\theta_{\mathfrak{E}},\partial_{X}]\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}\Psi_{\kappa,\alpha}}_{F_{2}}+\partial_{X}\underbrace{[\theta_{\mathfrak{E}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}]\Psi_{\kappa,\alpha}}_{h_{2}}\big)-\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}F_{\ell}^{\Omega}.

We use braces to label the various contributions to
h_{2}
and
F_{2}
.
Now
[\theta_{\mathfrak{E}},\partial]=\partial\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}
, and
\left|\partial_{T}\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}\right|\lesssim 1
while
\left|\partial_{X}\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}\right|\lesssim\nu^{1/4}
.
Using Lemma
7.7
, we thus find

\left|F_{2}\right|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta+1/4}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\leq 1}.

Commuting the cutoff through the multilinear operators, we find
[\theta_{\mathfrak{E}},\mathcal{A}]\lesssim\left|\mathcal{A}\right|
and
[\theta_{\mathfrak{E}},\mathcal{B}]\lesssim\left|\mathcal{B}\right|+\nu^{1/4}\left|\mathcal{A}\right|,
with the second term due to the derivative in
\mathcal{B}
.
Thus Lemma
7.7
yields

h_{2}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\leq 1}.

When we add (
7.18
) and (
7.19
), we obtain the difference
F_{3}\coloneqq(\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}-\theta_{\mathfrak{E}})F_{\ell}^{\Omega}
.
This is supported where
\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1
and hence
\mathfrak{D}\lesssim\nu^{-1/12}
.
Using Proposition
5.1
and (
7.12
), we can therefore compute

F_{3}\lesssim_{\ell}\nu^{1/4-o_{\ell}(1)}\mathfrak{D}^{\ell-1}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1}\lesssim_{\ell}\nu^{1/4-(\ell+1)/12+1/6-o_{\ell}(1)}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1}\lesssim_{\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta+1/4}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1}.

Adding (
7.18
) and (
7.19
), we can thus write

(7.20)

\nu^{1/4}\partial_{T}{\Omega}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}=\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell\\
\left|\kappa\right|\leq K\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{\kappa,\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell-1\\
\left|\kappa\right|\leq K-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{\kappa,\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\partial_{X}(h_{1}+h_{2})+F_{1}+F_{2}+F_{3}

for errors
h_{i}
and
F_{i}
that are suitably small.

For the shocking component, we collect all the
\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}
-commutators from
\Sigma_{0,\ell}
in
G_{1}
and the
\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}
-commutators from
\Sigma_{[1,K],\ell}
in
G_{2}
.
The former are order
\nu^{o_{\ell}(1)}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{D}\leq 1}
, and the latter are comparable to the size of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
in the relevant sums.
Using (iii) and (iv) in Lemma
7.7
and accounting for the factor of
\mathfrak{D}^{-3}
from
\partial_{X}
, we find

G_{1}+G_{2}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-2}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1}.

Let
G_{3}\coloneqq(\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}-\theta_{\mathfrak{E}})F_{\ell}^{\Sigma}
and note that
\theta_{\mathfrak{D}}-\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}
is supported where
\mathfrak{D}\gtrsim 1
and
\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1
, so
\mathfrak{D}\lesssim\nu^{-1/12}
.
Using Proposition
5.1
, we can compute
F_{\ell}^{\Sigma}\lesssim_{\ell}\nu^{1/4-o_{\ell}(1)}\mathfrak{D}^{\ell}
, so (
7.12
) yields

G_{3}\lesssim_{\ell}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-2}\nu^{1/4-(\ell+1)/12-1/12-o_{\ell}(1)}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-2}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\lesssim 1}.

Then we can write

(7.21)

\partial_{T}{\Sigma}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}=\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell+1\\
\left|\kappa\right|\leq K\end{subarray}}}\,\partial_{X}\mathcal{A}_{1}{\Psi}_{\kappa,\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell\\
\left|\kappa\right|\leq K-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{1}{\Psi}_{\kappa,\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{3}.

We now turn to the algebraic residue arising from the action of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
on partial sums.
For a given
\alpha
, the inner equations (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) involve
\mathcal{A}{\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}
while (
7.20
) and (
7.21
) feature
\sum_{\left|\kappa\right|\leq K}\mathcal{A}{\Psi}_{\kappa,\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}
(and similarly for
\mathcal{B}
).
If we expand the former, we will find terms with
\left|\kappa\right|>K
not contained in the latter.
We group these terms into
h_{3}
for
\Omega
and
G_{4}
for
\Sigma
:

h_{3}\coloneqq\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell\\
\kappa_{j}\leq K\\
\left|\kappa\right|>K\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell-1\\
\kappa_{j}\leq K\\
\left|\kappa\right|>K-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}},

and

G_{4}\coloneqq\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell+1\\
\kappa_{j}\leq K\\
\left|\kappa\right|>K\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{1}{\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}+\partial_{X}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\#\alpha=\ell\\
\kappa_{j}\leq K\\
\left|\kappa\right|>K-1\end{subarray}}}\,\mathcal{B}_{1}{\Psi}_{[K],\alpha}^{\textnormal{cut}}.

Now Lemma
7.7
yields

h_{3}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1},

and

G_{4}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-2}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}.

We are able to cut off
\mathfrak{E}
and
\mathfrak{D}
in these bounds because
\left|\kappa\right|>K>0
in the above sums, so each product
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
contains at least one factor of
\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}
.
Hence
h_{3}
and
G_{4}
are supported where
\mathfrak{E}\gtrsim 1/2
.

There is one final source of error in the shocking component: (
7.5
) involves advection by
{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}
.
We have implicitly replaced this by
{\Sigma}_{[K],0}^{\textnormal{cut}}
in the above sums.
Therefore let
G_{5}\coloneqq-\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}(P_{K,0}\Psi_{[K],\ell})
, which accounts for this difference.
Writing
\Psi_{[K],\ell}=\Psi_{0,\ell}+\Psi_{[1,K],\ell}
and using Propositions
5.1
,
5.10
, and
7.2
, we find

\displaystyle G_{5}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-o_{\ell}(1)}\mathfrak{D}^{\ell-4K-5}+\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\mathfrak{E}^{-1}\mathfrak{D}^{-4K-6}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}[\nu^{-o_{\ell}(1)}\mathfrak{D}^{\ell-1}+\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}]\mathfrak{E}^{-2K-1}\mathfrak{D}^{-2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}\mathfrak{E}^{-2K-1}\mathfrak{D}^{-2}.

Here we have used
\mathfrak{D}\lesssim\nu^{1/4-\beta/3}
and
2(1/4-\beta/3)>\eta
.

If we now set
h^{\textnormal{res}}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{3}h_{i}
,
F^{\textnormal{res}}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{3}F_{i}
, and
G\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{5}G_{i}
, (
7.16
) and (
7.17
) hold with the desired bounds for
m=n=0
.
Higher derivative estimates require repeated commutation with
\theta_{\mathfrak{E}}
, but the outcome is little changed.
∎

7.3.
Matching estimates

Throughout this section, we inductively assume that Proposition
7.1
holds for all
\ell^{\prime}<\ell
.
Combining Lemma
7.5
and
7.6
, we can rewrite (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) as

(7.22)

\displaystyle[\nu^{1/4}\partial_{T}+A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}(0)\partial_{X}]P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}

\displaystyle=\partial_{X}h+\hat{F},

(7.23)

\displaystyle\partial_{T}P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma}+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}({\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma})-B_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}^{2}P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=G+G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}

with

(7.24)

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1},

\displaystyle\hat{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta+1/4}\textup{{1}}_{\mathfrak{E}\leq 1},

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-2}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}

satisfying analogous derivative bounds.

We first treat the nonshocking component
P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}
.
By construction,
P^{\Omega}|_{\Gamma_{\beta}}=0
.
Because the right side of (
7.22
) is small, we can integrate (
7.22
) from
\Gamma_{\beta}
to show that
P^{\Omega}
itself is small.
For convenience, we transform (
5.17
) of Lemma
5.8
into the inner coordinates.

Lemma 7.8
.

For all
2\leq I^{\prime}\leq N
and
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
\int(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-r}\circ\gamma_{I^{\prime}}\lesssim_{b}\nu^{-1/4}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-r+1}
.

With this, we can easily treat the nonshocking component.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
for
\Omega
.

Applying Lemma
5.7
, we let
R\coloneqq\mathbf{R}_{0}[A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}(0),h]
and write (
7.22
) as

[\nu^{1/4}\partial_{T}+A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}(0)\partial_{X}](P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}-R)=-\nu^{1/4}A_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}(0)^{-1}\partial_{t}h+\hat{F}\eqqcolon F,

which satisfies
F\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta+1/4}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-2}
.
Integrating from zero data on
\Gamma_{\beta}
, Lemma
7.8
implies that

P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}-R\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta+1/4}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-1}.

Now
R
is a linear transformation of
h
, so (
7.24
) yields (
7.3
) for
P_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}
with
m=n=0
.
We inductively control
\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}p_{K,\ell}^{\Omega}
using the operator
\mathbf{R}_{n}
from Lemma
5.7
to renormalize.
We bound the derivative data using the procedure described in Step 1 of the proof of Proposition
5.10
.
Then the desired estimates follow from Lemma
7.8
.
∎

For the shocking component
P^{\Sigma}
, we exploit the scalar nature of the advection-diffusion equation (
7.23
) to control
P^{\Sigma}
via the comparison principle.
We set
P^{\Sigma}=0
below a certain time and use
G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}
to suppress the force near
\Gamma_{\beta}
.
This simplifies the analysis of higher derivatives.

Far from the origin, the shocking component is dominated by the advective derivative

(7.25)

\overline{\partial}_{T}\coloneqq\partial_{T}+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\Sigma_{0,0}\partial_{X}.

For our comparison argument, we require a lower bound on its action on
\mathfrak{E}
.

Lemma 7.9
.

We have
|\overline{\partial}_{T}\mathfrak{E}|\geq\mathfrak{E}\mathfrak{D}^{-2}
.

Proof.

Scaling to the outer coordinates, it suffices to show that
\mathfrak{d}^{2}\mathfrak{e}|\overline{\partial}_{t}\mathfrak{e}|\geq\mathfrak{e}^{2}
.
Using (
2.3
), we write

\overline{\partial}_{t}=\partial_{t}+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\sigma_{0,0}\partial_{x}=\partial_{t}-a\mathfrak{u}\partial_{x},

with
a
from (
2.3
).
Hence
\overline{\partial}_{t}\mathfrak{e}^{2}=\overline{\partial}_{t}(t^{2}+x^{2})=2t-2ax\mathfrak{u}.

The constants
a
and
b
in (
2.3
) have the same sign, so (
2.3
) implies that
ax\mathfrak{u}\geq 0
and

\mathfrak{e}|\overline{\partial}_{t}\mathfrak{e}|=\frac{1}{2}\left|\overline{\partial}_{t}\mathfrak{e}^{2}\right|=\left|t\right|+|ax\mathfrak{u}|.

Thus (
2.3
) and (
2.5
) yield

\mathfrak{d}^{2}\mathfrak{e}|\overline{\partial}_{t}\mathfrak{e}|=(\left|t\right|+3a^{-1}b\mathfrak{u}^{2})(\left|t\right|+|ax\mathfrak{u}|)\geq t^{2}+\left|x\right|\left|-at\mathfrak{u}+b\mathfrak{u}^{3}\right|=\mathfrak{e}^{2}.\qed

We use the comparison principle to control the shocking component via the following majorant.

Lemma 7.10
.

For all
r>1
and
s\geq 0
, there exists an increasing uniformly bounded Lipschitz function
\kappa\colon(-\infty,0]\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
Q\coloneqq\kappa(T)(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-r}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3s}
satisfies

(7.26)

\displaystyle\partial_{T}Q+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0){\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}\partial_{X}Q+(s+1)\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)(\partial_{X}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}})Q-B_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}^{2}Q

\displaystyle\geq(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-r}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-(3s+2)}.

Proof.

There exists
T_{1}<0
such that
\mathfrak{E},\mathfrak{D}\geq 1
where
T\leq T_{1}
.
First consider
T\leq T_{1}
.

By Proposition
7.2
,
{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}=\Sigma_{0,0}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{D}^{-3})
.
Using (
7.25
), we can write

\displaystyle\partial_{T}Q+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0){\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}\partial_{X}Q

\displaystyle+(s+1)\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)(\partial_{X}{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}})Q

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}_{T}Q-(s+1)\mathfrak{D}^{-2}Q+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{D}^{-6})Q+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{D}^{-3})\partial_{X}Q.

We can readily verify that
\partial_{X}Q\lesssim\mathfrak{D}^{-3}Q
and
\partial_{X}^{2}Q\lesssim\mathfrak{D}^{-6}Q
, so there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{T}Q+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0){\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}\partial_{X}Q

\displaystyle+(s+1)\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)(\partial_{X}{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}})Q-B_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}^{2}Q

\displaystyle\geq\overline{\partial}_{T}Q-(s+1)\mathfrak{D}^{-2}Q-C\left|T\right|^{-3}Q.

Recall that
\mathfrak{D}^{2}=\left|T\right|+3a^{-1}b\Sigma_{0,0}^{2}
and
\overline{\partial}_{T}\Sigma_{0,0}=0
, so
\overline{\partial}_{T}\mathfrak{D}^{2}=-1
.
Multiplying by the integrating factor
\mathfrak{D}^{2(s+1)}
, we therefore wish to arrange

(7.27)

\overline{\partial}_{T}\big(\mathfrak{D}^{2(s+1)}Q\big)-C\left|T\right|^{-3}\mathfrak{D}^{2(s+1)}Q\geq\mathfrak{E}^{-r}\mathfrak{D}^{-s}.

Let
\kappa(T)\coloneqq K\exp\big(\frac{C}{2\left|T\right|^{2}}\big)
for
T<T_{1}
and
K>0
to be determined, so
\dot{\kappa}=C\left|T\right|^{-3}\kappa
and

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{T}\big(\mathfrak{D}^{2(s+1)}Q\big)-C\left|T\right|^{-3}\mathfrak{D}^{2(s+1)}Q

\displaystyle=\kappa\overline{\partial}_{T}(\mathfrak{E}^{-r}\mathfrak{D}^{2-s})

\displaystyle=r\kappa\mathfrak{E}^{-r-1}\mathfrak{D}^{2-s}\left|\partial_{T}\mathfrak{E}\right|-\frac{\kappa}{2}(2-s)\mathfrak{E}^{-r}\mathfrak{D}^{-s}.

Using Lemma
7.9
, and
s\geq 0
, we find

\overline{\partial}_{T}\big(\mathfrak{D}^{2(s+1)}Q\big)-C\left|T\right|^{-3}\mathfrak{D}^{2(s+1)}Q\geq\kappa(r-1)\mathfrak{E}^{-r}\mathfrak{D}^{-s}.

Since
r>1
, (
7.27
) follows if we choose
K\coloneqq(r-1)^{-1}
.

Now consider
T\in[T_{1},0]
.
We can readily check that

|\partial_{X}({\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}Q)|+|\partial_{X}^{2}Q|+(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-r}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-(3s+2)}\lesssim Q,

Hence a multiplier of the form
\kappa(T)=\kappa(T_{1})\mathrm{e}^{\Lambda(T-T_{1})}
with
\Lambda\gg 1
ensures (
7.26
).
∎

We can finally construct the shocking component of the inner expansion.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
for
\Sigma
.

Recall the cutoff
\theta
from the beginning of the section, which is
0
on
[0,1/2]
and
1
on
[1,\infty)
.
Let
\theta_{\beta}\coloneqq\theta\circ\big(\mathsf{R}^{-1}\nu^{1/2-\beta}\mathfrak{E}\big)
, which is
1
where
\mathfrak{E}\geq\mathsf{R}\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)}
and
0
where
\mathfrak{E}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)}
.
Now fix
K,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Recalling (
7.23
), let
G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}\coloneqq-\theta_{\beta}G
.
Then
P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma}
solves

\partial_{T}P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma}+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}({\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma})-B_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}^{2}P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma}=(1-\theta_{\beta})G.

The forcing
(1-\theta_{\beta})G
vanishes where
\mathfrak{E}\geq\mathsf{R}\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)}
, and in particular where
T\leq-\mathsf{R}\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)}
.
We let
P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma}
be identically zero at such times.
We can interpret this as a Cauchy problem on the line with zero data at
T=-\mathsf{R}\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)}
.
We observe that
\theta_{\beta}
vanishes where
\mathfrak{E}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)}
, or equivalently
\mathfrak{e}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}
.
So
G_{\ell}^{\textnormal{m}}
satisfies the support condition in Proposition
7.1
.

We induct on the number of derivatives.
Fix
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and suppose we have shown Proposition
7.1
for all
(m^{\prime},n^{\prime})
with
n^{\prime}<n
or
n^{\prime}=n
and
m^{\prime}<m
.
Let
Z\coloneqq\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma}
.
When we apply
\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{X}^{n}
to (
7.23
), we must expand the product in the advection and commute derivatives with
\theta_{\beta}
.
The product terms have the form
\partial_{X}^{n+1-n^{\prime}}{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}\cdot\partial_{X}^{n^{\prime}}P_{K,\ell}^{\Sigma}
for
n^{\prime}\leq n+1
.
When
n^{\prime}<n
, we can use the inductive hypothesis and (
7.6
) to fold these into the forcing.
For the commutators, we observe that
\partial_{T}\theta_{\beta}\lesssim\nu^{1/2-\beta}\lesssim\mathfrak{E}^{-1}
and
\partial_{X}\theta_{\beta}\lesssim\nu^{3/4-\beta}\lesssim\mathfrak{D}^{-3}
, as both are supported where
\mathfrak{E}\asymp\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)}
and hence
\mathfrak{D}^{3}\lesssim\nu^{-1/4}\mathfrak{E}\lesssim\nu^{-(3/4-\beta)}
.
That is, these commutators contribute the proper factors of
\mathfrak{E}^{-1}
and
\mathfrak{D}^{-3}
.
In sum, we obtain

\partial_{T}Z+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0){\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}\partial_{X}Z+(n+1)\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)(\partial_{X}{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}})Z-B_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}^{2}Z=H

for
H\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-m-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n-2}
.
By Lemma
7.10
and linearity, there exists
0<Q\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)+\eta}(\mathfrak{E}\vee 1)^{-2K-m-1}(\mathfrak{D}\vee 1)^{-3n}
such that

\partial_{T}Q+\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0){\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}\partial_{X}Q+(n+1)\partial_{1}A_{1}^{1}(0)(\partial_{X}{\Sigma}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}})Q-B_{1}^{1}(0)\partial_{X}^{2}Q\geq H.

Because
Z(-\mathsf{R}\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)},\,\cdot\,)\equiv 0
, the comparison principle on
[-\mathsf{R}\nu^{-(1/2-\beta)},0]\times\mathbb{R}
implies that
Z\leq Q
.
Replacing
Q
by
-Q
, a symmetric argument yields
Z\geq-Q
, so
\left|Z\right|\leq Q
.
The proposition follows from induction.
∎

We next express these estimates in outer coordinates.

Proof of Corollary
7.3
.

Converting (
7.3
) to outer coordinates, we find

\big|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}({\psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}-{\psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}})\big|\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{K+1}\nu^{(\ell+1)\beta/3-2\beta/3-\varepsilon\beta}(\mathfrak{e}\vee\nu^{1/2})^{-2K-m-1}(\mathfrak{d}\vee\nu^{1/4})^{-3n}.

This implies (
7.7
).
Proposition
7.1
and Lemma
7.4
imply that
{\Psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}}
dominates
{\Psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}
, so (
7.11
) implies (
7.8
).

Next, in
\mathcal{I}_{\beta}\setminus\{\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}\}
we have
\mathfrak{e}^{-1}\asymp\nu^{-\beta}
and
\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\lesssim\mathfrak{e}^{-1/2}\asymp\nu^{-\beta/2}
.
We can thus simplify the previous display to

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}({\psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}-{\psi}_{[K],\ell}^{\textnormal{cut}})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{K+1}\nu^{(\ell+1)\beta/3-2\beta/3-\varepsilon\beta}\nu^{-2\beta K-m\beta-\beta}\nu^{-3n\beta/2},

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\beta,K,\ell,m,n}\nu^{(1-2\beta)K}\nu^{(\ell+1)\beta}\nu^{1-(2/3+\varepsilon+m+1+3n/2)\beta}.

Since
\varepsilon\leq 1/6
and
\beta<1/2
,

1-(2/3+\varepsilon+m+1+3n/2)\beta>1-(m+3n/2+11/6)/2>-m/2-3n/4

and (
7.9
) follows.
∎

Finally, we show that the partial outer sum
{\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}
matches the partial inner sum
{\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}\coloneqq\sum_{\ell=0}^{L}{\psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}
to high order in the matching zone
\mathfrak{e}\asymp\nu^{\beta}
when
K,L\gg 1
.

Proposition 7.11
.

For all
K,L\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

(7.28)

|\partial_{x}^{n}({\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}-{\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}})|\lesssim_{K,L,n}(\nu^{(1-2\beta)K}+\nu^{L\beta/3+1/4})\nu^{-3n/4}

where
\frac{\mathsf{R}}{4}\nu^{\beta}\leq\mathfrak{e}\leq\frac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}.

Proof.

First consider
n=0
.
Writing

{\psi}_{K}^{\textnormal{out}}-{\psi}_{L}^{\textnormal{in}}=({\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}-\psi_{[K,L]})-({\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}-\psi_{[K,L]}),

we reduce to matching between the outer and inner expansions and the grid.
Using Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
, the outer difference is dominated by

{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{out}}-\psi_{0,[L]}\lesssim_{L}\left|\log\nu\right|^{L}\nu^{(L+2)\beta/3}.

By Propositions
7.1
and
7.2
, the inner difference is dominated by

{\psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}-\psi_{[K],0}\lesssim_{K}\nu^{(1-2\beta)(K+1)}.

Combining and simplifying these expressions, we find (
7.28
).
Derivative estimates follow because
\mathfrak{d}^{-3}\lesssim\nu^{-3\beta/2}<\nu^{-3/4}
in this region.
∎

7.4.
Approximate inner solution

To close this section, we show that the inner partial sum is a good approximate solution of (
1.1
) where
\mathfrak{e}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}
provided
L\gg 1
.

Proposition 7.12
.

Fix
\beta\in(6/13,1/2)
.
For all
L\in\mathbb{N}
,

\partial_{t}{\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}+A({\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}})\partial_{x}{\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}=\nu\partial_{x}\big[B({\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}\partial_{x}{\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}})]+{F}_{L}^{\textnormal{in}}\quad\text{in }\mathfrak{e}\leq\frac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}

for a force
{F}_{L}^{\textnormal{in}}
satisfying
|\partial_{x}^{n}{F}_{L}^{\textnormal{in}}|\lesssim_{L,n}\nu^{L\beta/3-3n/4-1/2}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Proof.

We begin in the inner coordinates by substituting
{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{L}\nu^{\ell/4}{\Psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}
in (
4.1
) and controlling the residual error.

Propositions
7.1
and
7.2
imply that
{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}
is dominated by its first term:

{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}\lesssim_{L}\sup_{\{\mathfrak{e}\leq\mathsf{R}\nu^{\beta}/2\}}|{\Psi}_{0}^{\textnormal{in}}|\lesssim\sup_{\{\mathfrak{e}\leq\mathsf{R}\nu^{\beta}/2\}}\left|\mathfrak{D}\right|\lesssim\nu^{-(1/4-\beta/3)}.

We now write
A(\nu^{1/4}{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}})\partial_{X}{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}=\nu^{-1/4}\partial_{X}[f(\nu^{1/4}{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}})]
.
Expanding the flux about
0
, we find

\nu^{-1/4}[f(\nu^{1/4}{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}})-f(0)]=\nu^{-1/4}\sum_{r=0}^{L+1}\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r}A(0)}{(r+1)!}(\nu^{1/4}{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}})^{r+1}+\mathcal{O}_{L}(\nu^{(L+3)\beta/3-1/4}).

Moreover,

(7.29)

\nu^{-1/4}\sum_{r=0}^{L+1}\frac{\mathrm{D}^{r}A(0)}{(r+1)!}(\nu^{1/4}{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}})^{r+1}=\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{j}\leq L\\
\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|\leq L+1\end{subarray}}}\,\nu^{(\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|)/4}\mathcal{A}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}+\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{j}\leq L\\
\#\alpha\leq L+1\\
\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|>L+1\end{subarray}}}\,\nu^{(\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|)/4}\mathcal{A}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}.

Now Proposition
7.1
implies that
{\Psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}\lesssim_{\ell}\nu^{-(\ell+1)(1/4-\beta/3)}
, so

\nu^{(\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|)/4}\mathcal{A}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}\lesssim_{\alpha}\nu^{(\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|)\beta/3+\beta/3-1/4}.

In the second sum on the right in (
7.29
),
\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|\geq L+2
, so

\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{j}\leq L\\
\#\alpha\leq L+1\\
\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|>L+1\end{subarray}}}\,\nu^{(\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|)/4}\mathcal{A}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}\lesssim_{L}\nu^{(L+2)\beta/3+\beta/3-1/4}\lesssim_{L}\nu^{L\beta/3+\beta-1/4}.

Finally, the first sum on the right in (
7.29
) is nearly equal to the sums of the
\mathcal{A}
terms in (
4.13
) and (
4.14
), suitably weighted by factors of
\nu^{1/4}
.
It only includes one extra term in the non-shocking component of the form
\nu^{(\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|)/4}\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}
with
\#\alpha=L+1
and
\alpha=(0,\ldots,0)
.
This term satisfies

\nu^{(\#\alpha+\left|\alpha\right|)/4}\mathcal{A}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[5]$\bm{\perp}$}}{\mbox{\smaller[6]$\bm{\perp}$}}}{\Psi}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{in}}\lesssim_{L}\nu^{(L+1)\beta/3+\beta/3-1/4}\lesssim\nu^{L\beta/3}.

This dominates the earlier errors.
Accounting for the additional
\nu^{-1/4}\partial_{X}
in (
4.1
), which contributes
\nu^{-1/4}
, we see that the advective residue from
{\Psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}
in (
4.1
) is controlled by
\nu^{L\beta/3-1/4}
.
A similar analysis of the diffusive residue due to
B
yields error of order
\nu^{L\beta/3+\beta/3-1/4}\lesssim\nu^{L\beta/3-1/4}
.
So the residue in the inner coordinates is bounded by
\nu^{L\beta/3-1/4}
.
Using
\partial_{T}=\nu^{1/2}\partial_{t}
and
\Psi=\nu^{-1/4}\psi
, this corresponds to
{F}_{L}^{\textnormal{in}}\lesssim_{L}\nu^{L\beta/3-1/2}
in the outer coordinates.
Derivative estimates follow as usual.
∎

8.
The approximate solution and nonlinear closure

We have shown how to construct
{\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}
where
\mathfrak{e}\gtrsim\nu^{\beta}
and
{\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}
where
\mathfrak{e}\lesssim\nu^{\beta}
that very nearly solve (
1.1
) on their respective domains and agree to high order on the overlap
\{\mathfrak{e}\asymp\nu^{\beta}\}
.
Interpolating between these, we now form a highly accurate approximate solution
{\psi}_{K,L}^{\textnormal{app}}
on the entire domain
(t_{0},0)\times\mathbb{R}
.
Drawing on local well-posedness theory, we show that the true solution
\psi^{(\nu)}
remains arbitrarily close to
{\psi}_{K,L}^{\textnormal{app}}
in any Sobolev norm provided
K,L
are sufficiently large.

We have defined the outer and inner partial sums

{\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\nu^{k}{\psi}_{k}^{\textnormal{out}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{L}{\psi}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}.

Recalling (
4.19
) and the cutoff
\theta
from Section
7
, define
\zeta\coloneqq\theta\big(\tfrac{2\mathfrak{e}}{\mathsf{R}\nu^{\beta}}\big)
.
Then
\zeta\equiv 1
where
\mathfrak{e}\geq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}
and
\zeta\equiv 0
where
\mathfrak{e}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{4}\nu^{\beta}
.
Given
K,L\in\mathbb{N}
, we define

(8.1)

{\psi}_{K,L}^{\textnormal{app}}\coloneqq\zeta{\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}+(1-\zeta){\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}.

Note that
1-\zeta
is supported where
\mathfrak{e}\leq\tfrac{\mathsf{R}}{2}\nu^{\beta}
.
By Proposition
7.1
,
{\Sigma}_{\ell}^{\textnormal{in}}
solves (
4.14
) exactly in this region.
This underpins the precise choice of the constant in the cutoff
\zeta
.
Before proceeding, we record a simple bound on
{\psi}^{\textnormal{app}}
.

Lemma 8.1
.

Fix
\beta\in(6/13,1/2)
.
For all
K,L\in\mathbb{N}
and
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\big|\partial_{t}^{m}\partial_{x}^{n}{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}\big|\lesssim_{K,L,m,n}(\mathfrak{d}\vee\nu^{1/4})^{-3n+1}(\mathfrak{e}\vee\nu^{1/2})^{-m}\quad\text{in }[t_{0},0]\times[-1,1].

Proof.

Proposition
3.2
and Corollary
7.3
establish this bound for
{\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}
and
{\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}
, respectively.
Because
\left|\mathrm{D}\zeta\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{e}^{-1}
, derivatives landing on the cutoff do not disrupt the estimate.
∎

We wish to show that
\psi^{(\nu)}\approx{\psi}_{K,L}^{\textnormal{app}}
when
K,L\gg 1
and
\nu\ll 1
.
To see this, we must treat the full nonlinear system (
1.1
).
This is not trivial because we permit the diffusion matrix
B
to be degenerate.
In this situation, it is necessary to impose additional structural assumptions to ensure local well-posedness.
These conditions go back to foundational work of Kawashima and Shizuta
[
46
,
70
]
.
Here, we work in a related framework of Serre
[
69
,
68
]
.

In
(H1)
, we assume that there is a smooth entropy-flux pair
(\eta,q)
such that
\eta
is strictly convex.
That is,
\eta,q\colon\mathcal{V}\to\mathbb{R}
,
\nabla\eta=(\nabla q)^{\top}A
, and
\mathrm{D}^{2}\eta>0
.
(In fact we only need this pair in a connected neighborhood containing the range of
\psi^{(\nu)}
; we suppress this point below.)
We interpret the entropy gradient
\nabla\eta\colon\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{V}
as a change of unknown.
Convexity ensures that its Jacobian does not vanish, so it is a diffeomorphism.

Next, in
1
we assume that the entropy is
strongly dissipated
by the diffusion
B
.
That is, if we interpret
\mathrm{D}^{2}\eta
as a bilinear form on
\mathcal{V}
,

(8.2)

\mathrm{D}^{2}\eta\big(\xi,B\xi\big)\gtrsim\left|B\xi\right|^{2}\quad\text{for all }\xi\in\mathcal{V}.

Informally,
\eta
experiences dissipation whenever
\psi
does.

Finally, we assume that
B
has constant rank
N-s
for some
s\in[N]
and there exists a fixed basis of
\mathcal{V}
(different from that used above) in which the first
s
rows of
B
vanish identically.
For example, in the full Navier–Stokes system (
1.2
), there is dissipation in momentum and energy but not mass, so
s=1
.
We work in this basis in the remainder of the section, and thus set aside the distinction between shocking and non-shocking components.
As shown in
[
69
]
, these hypotheses generalize and simplify the classical Kawashima–Shizuta conditions
[
46
,
70
]
for local well-posedness.
They similarly yield local well-posedness
[
68
]
, and we use related
a priori
estimates to show that
{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}\approx\psi^{(\nu)}
.

In combination, (
8.2
) and the constant range of
B
imply that (
1.1
) becomes more favorable if we transform the range of
B
under the entropic diffeomorphism
\nabla\eta
.
To this end, let
\Pi
denote projection onto the last
N-s
components in the basis fixed above, and let
\iota\colon\mathbb{R}^{N-s}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
denote the corresponding embedding.
We define a diffeomorphism
\mathsf{H}\colon\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{V}
by
\mathsf{H}\psi\coloneqq(1-\Pi)\psi+\Pi\nabla\eta
.
This preserves the components of
\psi
that do not directly experience diffusion and entropically transforms those that do.
Then let

\varphi^{(\nu)}\coloneqq\mathsf{H}\psi^{(\nu)},\quad{\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}\coloneqq\mathsf{H}{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}},\quad\text{and}\quad v\coloneqq\varphi^{(\nu)}-{\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}.

We will show that
v\ll 1
provided
K,L\gg 1
.
Because
\mathsf{H}
is a diffeomorphism, it will follow that
\psi^{(\nu)}-{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}\asymp v\ll 1
.

To begin, we show that
v
solves an equation with very small forcing.
We first focus on the equation for
\varphi^{(\nu)}
.
In
[
68
]
, Serre shows:

Proposition 8.2
.

There exist smooth matrix-valued functions
S_{0},S_{1}\colon\mathcal{V}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
{Z\colon\mathcal{V}\to\mathbb{R}^{N-s}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{N-s}}
such that
S_{0}
and
S_{1}
are symmetric,
S_{0}
is positive-definite and
(s,N-s)
-block diagonal,
\langle Z\xi,\xi\rangle\gtrsim\left|\xi\right|^{2}
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N-s}
, and

(8.3)

S_{0}\big(\varphi^{(\nu)}\big)\partial_{t}\varphi^{(\nu)}+S_{1}\big(\varphi^{(\nu)}\big)\partial_{x}\varphi^{(\nu)}=\nu\iota\partial_{x}Z\big(\varphi^{(\nu)}\big)\partial_{x}\Pi\varphi^{(\nu)}.

The change of unknown
\mathsf{H}
is advantageous because it fixes the
kernel
of the diffusion in addition to its range.
As a result, the diffusion becomes block diagonal and coercive in the last
N-s
components.
This prevents derivatives of
Z
from “leaking” into its kernel, where they cannot be controlled.
The price we pay for the change of variables is the nonlinear matrix
S_{0}
acting on
\partial_{t}
.
Nonetheless, because
\eta
is a convex entropy for the hyperbolic system, we can arrange that
S_{0}
is PSD and
S_{1}
is symmetric.
These properties enable our energy estimates.

To control
v
, we make use of the following
W^{1,\infty}
-bootstrap.
Given
t_{\mathrm{b}}\in(t_{0},0]
, we say
v
satisfies the bootstrap on
[t_{0},t_{\mathrm{b}}]
if

(8.4)

\left\|v\right\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(t)+\left\|\mathrm{D}v\right\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(t)\leq 1\quad\text{for all }t\in[t_{0},t_{\mathrm{b}}].

In particular, the functions
\varphi^{(\nu)}
and
{\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}
map to a compact subset of
\mathcal{V}
, which may depend on
\beta
,
K
, and
L
but not on
\nu
.

With this bootstrap, we can check that
{\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}
approximately solves (
8.3
).

Proposition 8.3
.

Fix
\beta\in(6/13,1/2)
and let
S_{0},S_{1}
, and
Z
be as in Proposition
8.2
.
Then for all
K,L\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a force
F
such that

(8.5)

S_{0}\big({\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}\big)\partial_{t}{\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}+S_{1}\big({\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}\big)\partial_{x}{\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}=\nu\iota\partial_{x}Z\big({\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}\big)\partial_{x}\Pi{\varphi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}+F

and
\partial_{x}^{n}F\lesssim_{K,L,n}[\nu^{(1-2\beta)K-3/4}+\nu^{L\beta/3-1/2}]\nu^{-3n/4}
on
[t_{0},t_{\mathrm{b}}]\times\mathbb{R}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Proof.

In
[
68
]
, the transformation from (
1.1
) to (
8.3
) consists of multiplication by a smooth matrix-valued function
S
of the solution.
By (
8.4
),
S
is uniformly bounded.
It thus suffices to show that

(8.6)

\partial_{t}{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}+A({\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}})\partial_{x}{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}=\nu\partial_{x}[B({\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}})\partial_{x}{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}]+\hat{F}

with
\partial_{x}^{n}\hat{F}\lesssim_{K,L,n}\nu^{(1-2\beta)K-1/4-3n/4}
.
This implies (
8.5
) for
n=0
, and Lemma
8.1
implies that spatial derivatives landing on
S({\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}})
do not alter the estimate.

We analyze (
8.6
) separately in the outer, inner, and matching regions, which roughly correspond to
\{\mathfrak{e}\gtrsim\nu^{\beta}\}
,
\{\mathfrak{e}\lesssim\nu^{\beta}\}
, and
\{\mathfrak{e}\asymp\nu^{\beta}\}
.
In the outer region,
{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}={\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}
, so by Proposition
3.3
,
\hat{F}={F}_{K}^{\textnormal{out}}
is suitably small.
In the inner region,
{\psi}_{[K,L]}^{\textnormal{app}}={\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}
and the conclusion likewise follows from Proposition
7.12
.

In the matching region, we perturb
{\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}}
by the small quantity
p\coloneqq\zeta({\psi}_{[L]}^{\textnormal{in}}-{\psi}_{[K]}^{\textnormal{out}})
.
We can thus decompose
\hat{F}
into
{F}_{K}^{\textnormal{out}}
plus terms arising when derivatives land on
p
.
Because
\partial_{t}\zeta\lesssim\mathfrak{e}^{-1}
and
\partial_{x}\zeta\lesssim\mathfrak{e}^{-1}\zeta\lesssim\mathfrak{d}^{-3}\zeta
,
p
satisfies the usual derivative estimates.
